L2143: ## **Reviewer Comment and Assessment of the Exposure-TG Models**
L2144: The Applicant's exposure-TG model is acceptable and reasonably describes the individual observed TG concentrations and their variability in healthy volunteers, patients with FCS and patients with HTG and CVD.
L2145: - The PD parameters were estimated with an acceptable precision (&lt;30\% RSE), with a relatively higher relative standard error (%RSE) of 36\% for the estimated effect of FCS disease on EC_{50}. The estimated interindividual random error (\eta-) shrinkage was low (18\%) for EC_{50} and acceptable for the reliable graphical assessment of covariate effects. The \eta-shrinkages were relatively high for K_{out} (46\%) and E_{max} (42\%) parameters. The residual error (\epsilon-) shrinkage was low (&lt;3\%), indicating the informativeness of the GOF plots to diagnose structural and residual error model misspecifications. The GOF plots show that the exposure-TG model fitted the observed data well with no critical trends or bias in the predicted versus the observed concentrations of TG and in the CWRES versus predictions or time from all studies and olezarsen dose levels.
- The pcVPC plots, stratified by disease status, indicate that the Applicant's exposure-TG model reasonably captures and describes the observed median TG concentrations and their variability from all studies with minimal bias.
- The Applicant did not provide the rationale and physiological explanation for including ADA titer on EC_{50}. The removal of ADA titer on EC_{50} from the exposure-TG model did not significantly increase the objective function value (7.3 points for 1 df). The model without ADA status and titer effect did not change the estimated BSV for EC_{50} or the estimated PD model parameters (the estimated effect of FCS on EC_{50} increased by 13\%). Olezarsen EC_{50}
L2146: was estimated to 12 ng/mL in the model without ADA titer effect versus 10.6 ng/mL in the Applicant's final exposure-TG model.
L2147: ## 14.5.3. Application of the PK and Exposure-Response Models
L2148: The PK and exposure-response models were used to perform population simulations to support dose selection in patients with FCS. Simulations were performed for the following scenarios:
L2149: - 50 mg olezarsen Q4W for 13 doses (364 days) and every month (Q1M) for 12 doses (360 days).
- 80 mg olezarsen Q4W for 13 doses (364 days) and Q1M for 12 doses (360 days).
L2150: **Figure 62** shows a trend towards a marginally higher percentage change from baseline (CFB) in ApoC-III and TG for the 80 mg compared to the 50 mg doses, with overlapping 95% CIs for the percent CFB in TG concentrations.
L2151: **Figure 63** shows that the PK, ApoC-III, and TG profiles for Q4W and Q1M dosing overlap following first dose and at steady-state with a marginal difference in the percent CFB in ApoC-III and TG concentrations at the end of the dosing interval for the Q1M.

L2162: ## **Reviewer Comment**
L2163: The simulations from the PK and exposure-response models were reproducible and suggest that the 80 mg olezarsen administered Q4W or Q1M are acceptable and estimated to achieve, at the end of the dosing interval (C_{\text{trough,ss}}), a percent reduction from baseline in ApoC-III of 73.5% for Q4W or 71.6% for Q1M, and percent reductions from baseline in TG of 29.4% for Q4W and 28.6% for Q1M, in patients with FCS. According to the exposure-TG modeling and simulation, 50 mg and 80 mg olezarsen administered Q4W are estimated to achieve, at steady-state exposure and the end of the dosing interval, a median percent reduction from baseline in TG of 23.4 versus 29.4%, in patients with FCS. Figure 62 shows a trend towards a marginally higher percentage CFB in TG for the 80 mg doses, with overlapping 95% CIs for the percent CFB in TG concentrations.

L2185: ## 14.5.4.2. Exposure-Hypersensitivity Relationship
L2186: In Trial ISIS 678354-CS3 and -CS8, 10 patients (6.5%) experienced at least one treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAE) by hypersensitivity FDA Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities Query (FMQ) broad scope definition.
L2187: The reviewer assessment of the relationship between olezarsen exposure (AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss}, and C_{trough,ss}) and the occurrence of hypersensitivity TEAEs (broad scope), observed a slight trend between higher AUC_{ss} or C_{trough,ss} and the occurrence of hypersensitivity TEAEs. However, this
L2188: ## NDA 218614
## Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2189: trend was driven mostly by three patients receiving the 80 mg dose (Figure 66). No trend was observed between C_{max,ss} and the occurrence of hypersensitivity TEAEs.
L2190: Figure 66 shows that the steady-state exposure metrics are overlapping between patients who experienced hypersensitivity TEAEs and those who did not experience hypersensitivity TEAEs.
L2191: Three patients in the 80 mg treatment arm of Trial ISIS 678354-CS3 and -CS8, who have among the highest exposure metrics, were driving the positive relationship estimated between olezarsen AUC_{ss} or C_{trough,ss} and the occurrence of hypersensitivity TEAEs, according to a logistic regression analysis. Amongst these three patients, one patient had negative ADA titer, and two patients had ADA titers of 1600 and 3200, respectively.

L2200: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L2201: ## 15.1. Trial ISIS 678354-CS3
L2202: ## 15.1.1. Clinical Study Protocol Synopsis
L2203: The following is the Applicant's protocol synopsis of Trial ISIS 678354-CS3 from protocol amendment 9 (August 2, 2023), which was the final version of the protocol prior to the data cutoff.
L2204: ## Title
L2205: A Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Phase 3 Study of AKCEA-APOCIII-LRX Administered Subcutaneously to Patients With Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome (FCS)
L2206: ## Landmark Dates
L2207: - First subject Dosed: December 17, 2020
- Last subject Enrolled: July 11, 2022
- Last subject, Last Dose: June 20, 2023
- Last subject, Last Visit: Subjects still in follow-up time at data cut-off
- Data Cutoff: July 14, 2023
- Database Lock Date: August 30, 2023
L2208: ## Objectives
L2209: ## Primary
L2210: To evaluate the efficacy of olezarsen as compared to placebo on the percent change in fasting TG from baseline.
L2211: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2212: ## Secondary
L2213: To evaluate the efficacy of olezarsen as compared to placebo on the following:
L2214: - Percent change in fasting ApoC-III from baseline
- Proportion of subjects who achieve \ge 40\% reduction in fasting TG from baseline
- Percent change in fasting ApoB-48 from baseline
- Percent change in fasting non-HDL-C from baseline
- Adjudicated AP event rate in subjects with a prior history of pancreatitis within 10 years prior to screening
- Adjudicated AP event rate
- Proportion of subjects who achieve \ge 70\% reduction in fasting TG from baseline
- Proportion of subjects who achieve fasting TG \le 880 mg/dL (10 mmol/L)
- Adjudicated AP event rate in subjects with two or more events of adjudicated AP in 5 years prior to enrollment
- Proportion of subjects who achieve fasting TG \le 500 mg/dL (5.7 mmol/L)
L2215: ## Study Design
L2216: This is a multi-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study. Eligible subjects will enter an approximately 4-week, but no more than 8-week, screening period that includes an at least 2-week diet stabilization/run-in period for subjects not already on a stable diet, and an approximately 2-week qualification period. Following qualification, approximately 60 eligible subjects will be randomized 1:1 to Cohort A (50 mg) or Cohort B (80 mg) and each cohort further randomized 2:1 to receive olezarsen or placebo in a 53-week treatment period.
L2217: Subjects in Cohort A will receive 50 mg of olezarsen Q4W or matching volume of placebo (0.5 mL) for Weeks 1 through 49 of the treatment period. Subjects in Cohort B will receive 80 mg olezarsen Q4W or matching volume of placebo (0.8 mL) for Weeks 1 through 49 of the treatment period. Randomization will be stratified by: (1) prior history of pancreatitis (within 10 years prior to screening); and (2) previous treatment with volanesorsen. Dietary counseling will commence at the start of the diet stabilization period and will be reinforced at intervals throughout the treatment and follow-up period. Following the Week 53 visit, eligible subjects may elect to enroll in an open-label extension (OLE) study pending study approval by the institutional review board/independent ethics committee and the appropriate regulatory authority. Subjects not participating in the OLE will enter the 13-week post-treatment evaluation period. The primary endpoint for the study will be evaluated after the last subject has completed the Week 53/ET visit and will be based on the percent change in fasting TG from baseline at the primary analysis time point (Month 6).
L2218: The subjects, investigators, study staff, and the Applicant are to remain blinded to lipid data (central and local) starting after the first dose on Day 1 then throughout the remainder of the study.
L2219: ## Population
L2220: ## **Inclusion Criteria**
L2221: - Must have given written informed consent (signed and dated) and any authorizations required by local law and be able to comply with all study requirements
- Aged \ge 18 years at the time of informed consent
- A diagnosis of Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome (type 1 Hyperlipoproteinemia) by documentation of confirmed homozygote, compound heterozygote or double heterozygote for loss-of-function mutations in type 1-causing genes (such as LPL, GPIHBP1, ApoA5, ApoC2, GPD1, or LMF1)
- Fasting TG \ge 880 mg/dL (10 mmol/L) at screening. If fasting TG is &lt; 880 mg/dL up to two additional tests may be performed with any single test used to qualify
- History of pancreatitis (defined as a recorded diagnosis of AP or hospitalization or emergency room (ER) visit for severe abdominal pain consistent with AP and for which no alternate diagnosis was made within 10 years prior to screening. Subjects without a recorded history of pancreatitis, or no recorded history within 10 years prior to screening, are also eligible but their enrollment will be capped at 35% (i.e., \le 21 of the 60 planned subjects)
- Willing to follow a diet comprising \le 20 g fat per day during the study
- Willing to complete all patient-reported outcome assessments throughout the study
- Satisfy the following:
L2222: Females: must be non-pregnant and non-lactating and either:
L2223: Surgically sterile (e.g., tubal occlusion, hysterectomy, bilateral salpingectomy, bilateral oophorectomy);
L2224: Postmenopausal (defined as 12 months of spontaneous amenorrhea in females &gt; 55 years of age or, in females \le 55 years, 12 months of spontaneous amenorrhea without an alternative medical cause and FSH levels in the postmenopausal range for the laboratory involved);
L2225: Abstinent*; or
L2226: If engaged in sexual relations of child-bearing potential, agree to use a highly effective contraceptive method from the time of signing the informed consent form until at least 17 weeks after the last dose of study drug (olezarsen or placebo)
L2227: Males: Surgically sterile, abstinent*, or if engaged in sexual relations with a female of child-bearing potential, subject is utilizing a highly effective contraceptive method from the time of signing the informed consent form until at least 17 weeks after the last dose of study drug (olezarsen or placebo)
L2228: * *Abstinence is only acceptable as true abstinence, i.e., when this is in line with the preferred and usual lifestyle of the subject. Periodic abstinence (e.g., calendar, ovulation, symptothermal, post-ovulation methods), declaration of abstinence for the duration of a trial and withdrawal are not acceptable methods of contraception*
L2229: - Atypical antipsychotic medications (e.g., olanzapine and clozapine) will be allowed if on a stable dose for at least 3 months prior to screening and dose and regimen expected to remain constant through the end of their participation in this study
- The following concomitant medications will be allowed if on a stable dose for at least 4 weeks prior to screening and dose and regimen expected to remain constant through the end
L2230: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2231: of their participation in this study (occasional or intermittent use of over-the-counter (OTC) medications will be allowed at investigator's discretion):
L2232: Statins, omega-3 fatty acids (prescription and OTC), fibrates, or other lipid-lowering medications. Subjects taking OTC omega-3 fatty acids should make every effort to remain on the same brand through the end of the study
L2233: Antidiabetic medications
L2234: Antihypertensive medications
L2235: Oral anticoagulants (e.g., warfarin, dabigatran, rivaroxaban, and apixaban) and regular clinical monitoring is performed
L2236: Tamoxifen, estrogens or progestins
L2237: ## Exclusion Criteria
L2238: - Clinically significant abnormalities in medical history (e.g., previous acute coronary syndrome within 6 months of screening, major surgery within 3 months of screening) or physical examination
- AP within 4 weeks prior to screening
- Screening laboratory results as follows, or any other clinically significant abnormalities in screening laboratory values that would render a subject unsuitable for inclusion:Platelet count &lt;100K/mm^3 at screening or qualification. If the platelet count is &lt;100K/mm^3 up to two additional tests may be performed to qualify at both screening and qualification
- ALT or AST &gt;3.0 \times upper limit of normal (ULN)
- Total bilirubin &gt;ULN unless due to Gilbert's syndrome
- eGFR &lt;45 mL/min/1.73 m^2 (as determined by the Chronic Kidney Disease Epidemiology formula for creatinine clearance; (Levey et al. 2009))
- Urine protein/creatinine ratio (UPCR) \ge 500 mg/g or urine albumin/creatinine ratio (UACR) \ge 300 mg/g
- Uncontrolled arterial hypertension (blood pressure [BP] &gt;160/100 mmHg)
- History of bleeding diathesis or coagulopathy or clinically significant abnormality in coagulation parameters at screening
- Active infection requiring systemic antiviral or antimicrobial therapy that will not be completed prior to Study Day 1 or active Covid-19 infection with or without therapy that will not be resolved by Study Day 1
- Active infection with HIV, hepatitis C or hepatitis B diagnosed by initial serological testing and confirmed with RNA testing, or prior treatment for hepatitis C. Subjects at screening who test positive by serology, but negative by RNA may be allowed in consultation with the Sponsor Medical Monitor
- Malignancy within 5 years, except for basal or squamous cell carcinoma of the skin or carcinoma in situ of the cervix that has been successfully treated. Subjects with a history of
L2239: ## NDA 218614
## Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2240: other malignancies that have been treated with curative intent and which have no recurrence within 5 years may also be eligible if approved by the Sponsor Medical Monitor

- Blood donation of 50 to 499 mL within 30 days of screening or of &gt;499 mL within 60 days of screening
- Treatment with another investigational drug (non-oligonucleotide), biological agent, or device within 1 month of screening, or 5 half-lives of investigational agent, whichever is longer
- Previous treatment with an oligonucleotide (including small interfering ribonucleic acid [siRNA]) within 4 months of screening, or 5 half-lives, whichever is longer. This exclusion does not apply to vaccines
- Concomitant medication/procedure restrictions:Systemic corticosteroids or anabolic steroids within 6 weeks prior to screening and during the study unless approved by the Sponsor Medical Monitor
- Plasma apheresis within 4 weeks prior to screening or planned during the study
- Unwillingness to comply with study procedures, including follow-up, as specified by this protocol, or unwillingness to cooperate fully with the investigator
- Have any other conditions, which, in the opinion of the investigator would make the subject unsuitable for inclusion, or could interfere with the subject participating in or completing the study

L2269: ## Statistical Plan
L2270: ## Sample Size Considerations
L2271: Based upon prior clinical trial experience with FCS subjects, the SD of the percent change from baseline in TGs is approximately 46%. With 14 subjects in each olezarsen treatment group and 14 in the pooled placebo group, there would be a 90% power to detect a 60% difference between each olezarsen treatment group and pooled placebo group at an alpha level of 0.05 (two-sided), assuming 60% reduction in the olezarsen treatment subjects and no change in the placebo subjects. Approximately 60 subjects will be enrolled in this trial to account for potential early dropouts and to facilitate safety evaluations.
L2272: Eligible subjects will be randomized 1:1 to Cohort A or Cohort B and each cohort further randomized 2:1 (olezarsen: placebo) and stratified for:
L2273: - Prior history of pancreatitis within 10 years prior to screening* vs. no history of pancreatitis or no history within 10 years prior to screening
- Previous treatment with volanesorsen
L2274: * *History of pancreatitis is defined as a recorded diagnosis of AP or hospitalization or ER visit for severe abdominal pain consistent with AP and for which no alternate diagnosis was made*
L2275: ## Analysis of the Primary Endpoint
L2276: The primary analysis of the primary endpoint is to compare the percent CFB to the primary analysis time point in fasting TG between each olezarsen treatment group vs. pooled placebo using an analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) model in the full analysis set (FAS). The ANCOVA model will include the effects of treatment (olezarsen 80 mg, olezarsen 50 mg, or placebo), two
L2277: randomization stratification factors (history of pancreatitis and previous treatment with volanesorsen), and log-transformed baseline TG. To account for potential heterogeneity in variance, the 95% CI of the treatment differences will be calculated using the robust variance estimator based on the Bell and McCaffrey method (Imbens and Kolesar 2016). If subjects have intercurrent event(s) before Week 23, e.g., treatment discontinuation, use of additional medication, changes in background or concomitant treatments, the treatment policy strategy will be implemented, the subjects' assessments post intercurrent events will continue to be collected and utilized in the primary analysis. Subjects with a missing TG value after discontinuation will have TG value multiply imputed at primary analysis time point based on the known assessments from the retrieved dropouts (i.e., subjects who discontinue before Week 23 but still have TG assessments at Week 23, 25, or 27), if there is a sufficient number of retrieved dropouts. The imputation model will include the following variables: TG values at baseline and postbaseline visits and the two randomization stratification factors. The multiple imputation will be stratified by treatment group. If there is not a sufficient number of retrieved dropouts, missing TG value at the primary time point will be imputed using a "wash-out" (Jump to Reference, J2R) multiple imputation approach. With the approach, subjects treated with olezarsen who discontinue study will have missing post-discontinuation TG values multiply imputed based on the fasting TG at baseline, two randomization stratification factors, and the imputation model for the placebo group. Subjects in the placebo group who discontinue the study will have missing post-discontinuation TG values multiply imputed based on fasting TG values at baseline and postbaseline visits from the placebo group.
L2278: It is anticipated that the rate of missing data is low.
L2279: The null hypothesis of the primary analysis is that the percent CFB to primary analysis time point in fasting TG is the same between each olezarsen and placebo treatment groups. The alternative hypothesis is that the percent CFB to primary analysis time point in fasting TG is different between each olezarsen and placebo treatment groups.
L2280: The primary efficacy analysis will take place after the last subject has completed the Week 53/ET visit and the database has been locked, and will be based on the percent CFB in fasting TG at primary analysis time point (end of Month 6). Details of the controlled access to the unblinded data will be outlined in the statistical analysis plan (SAP) and unblinding plan.
L2281: During the database lock cleaning process, redacted source documentation may need to be shared securely with the site Clinical Research Associate for source data verification procedures. The following sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoint will be conducted to assess the robustness of the primary analysis result, and details of the analyses will be outlined in the SAP:
L2282: - The primary analysis described above will be repeated in the per-protocol set (PPS).
- The primary analysis will be repeated in the subset of FAS with no missing TG value at the baseline and primary analysis time point.
- Additional sensitivity analyses using controlled imputations (pattern mixture models) with ANCOVA will be conducted in the FAS to assess the robustness of the primary analysis results.For subjects treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study due to adverse event (AE) or lack of efficacy (i.e., informative missing), their missing TG data after treatment discontinuation will be multiply imputed using the copy increment from reference (CIR)
L2283: approach based on the estimates from of the placebo subjects. The CIR approach is detailed in (Carpenter et al. 2013). The assumption is when a subject discontinues treatment due to informative missing, from withdrawal onwards they would progress in the same way as the subjects in the placebo group. For subjects who discontinue due to other reasons and all subjects from the placebo group, their missing data will be treated as non-informative missing, and data will be multiply imputed based on the distribution of their own arm.
L2284: - All subjects treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study will have missing post-treatment discontinuation TG values multiply imputed using the CIR approach based on the estimates from the placebo subjects. Missing TG for placebo subjects will be imputed based on the distribution of the placebo group.
- A nonparametric Wilcoxon rank-sum test will be performed on the percent change in fasting TG from baseline to the primary analysis time point in the FAS. Missing data will be handled using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis.
- An additional sensitivity analysis using the two-dimensional tipping-point approach will be conducted in the FAS to assess the impact of missing data on the result of the primary analysis. A range of penalties will be added to the imputed values for each group until the result becomes non-significant and tipping points are identified.
- An ANCOVA model will be performed in the FAS using the nature log transformed data. Missing data will be handled using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis.
L2285: ## Analysis of Secondary Endpoints
L2286: - The percent change in fasting TG from baseline to Month 12 in the FAS will be analyzed in the same way as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint.
- The proportion of subjects who achieve \ge 40\% reduction in fasting TG from baseline to the primary analysis time point in the FAS will be compared between each olezarsen treatment group and placebo group using a logistic regression model with the treatment group, history of pancreatitis (yes/no), previous treatment with volanesorsen (yes/no) as the factors, and log-transformed baseline TG as a covariate. Missing TG values will be imputed using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. A sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the FAS, in which all subjects who have missing data and discontinue treatment before Week 23 will be considered as non-responders.
- The percent change in fasting ApoC-III, ApoB-48, and non-HDL-C from baseline to the primary analysis time point and Month 12 in the FAS will be analyzed in the same way as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint.
- The proportion of subjects who achieve fasting TG \le 880 mg/dL at the primary analysis time point will be analyzed in the same way as the proportion of subjects who achieve \ge 40\% reduction in fasting TG. Missing values will be imputed using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. It will be conducted in the subset of FAS with baseline TG &gt; 880 mg/dL. A sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the subset of
L2287: FAS with baseline TG &gt;880 mg/dL, in which all subjects who have missing data and discontinue treatment before Week 23 will be considered as non-responders.
L2288: - The following adjudicated AP event ratesDuring the treatment period (Week 1 through 53) in the subset of FAS with a prior history of pancreatitis within 10 years prior to screening
- During the treatment period (Week 1 through 53) in the FAS
- From Week 13 to 53 in the subset of FAS with a prior history of pancreatitis within 10 years prior to screening
- From Week 13 to 53 in the FAS
- During the treatment period (Week 1 through 53) in the subset of FAS with two or more events of adjudicated AP in 5 years prior to enrollment
- From Week 13 to 53 in the subset of FAS with two or more events of adjudicated AP in 5 years prior to enrollment
- Will be compared between pooled olezarsen treatment group and placebo group using a Poisson regression model or negative binomial regression model with the treatment group, history of pancreatitis (yes/no), previous treatment with volanesorsen (yes/no) as the factors, and number of adjudicated AP events in 5 years prior to the enrollment as a covariate. The logarithm of time in year that each subject observed during the corresponding analysis period (Week 13 to 53 or Week 1 to 53/ET) will be used as an offset variable.
- The proportion of subjects who achieve \ge 70\% reduction in fasting TG from baseline to the primary analysis time point in the FAS will be analyzed in the same way as the proportion of subjects who achieve \ge 40\% reduction in fasting TG. Missing values will be imputed using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. A sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the FAS, in which all subjects who have missing data and discontinue treatment before Week 23 will be considered as non-responders.
- The proportion of subjects who achieve fasting TG \le 500 mg/dL at the primary analysis time point will be analyzed in the same way as the proportion of subjects who achieve \ge 40\% reduction in fasting TG. Missing values will be imputed using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. It will be conducted in the subset of FAS with baseline TG &gt;500 mg/dL. A sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the subset of FAS with baseline TG &gt;500 mg/dL, in which all subjects who have missing data and discontinue treatment before Week 23 will be considered as non-responders.
L2289: ## 15.2. Trial ISIS 678354-CS8
L2290: The following is the Applicant's protocol synopsis of Trial ISIS 678354-CS8 from protocol amendment 2 (March 22, 2023), which was the final version of the protocol prior to the data cutoff.
L2291: ## Title
L2292: Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo-Controlled, Phase 2b Study of ISIS 678354 in Patients With Hypertriglyceridemia and Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease (Established or at Increased Risk for), and/or With Severe Hypertriglyceridemia
L2293: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2294: ## **Landmark Dates**
L2295: - First subject enrolled: June 1, 2022
- First subject dosed: June 1, 2022
- Last subject enrolled September 19, 2022
- Last subject, last dose: August 22, 2023
- Last subject, last visit: January 17, 2023
- Data cutoff: September 15, 2023
- Database Lock: October 20, 2023
L2296: ## **Objectives**
L2297: ## **Primary**
L2298: - To evaluate the effect of olezarsen on percent change in fasting TG levels as compared to placebo
L2299: ## **Secondary**
L2300: - To evaluate the effect of olezarsen as compared to placebo on:Percent change in fasting TG levels upon longer duration of treatment (1 year)
- Proportion of patients who achieve different thresholds in fasting TG
- Percent change in fasting ApoC-III, VLDL-C, remnant cholesterol, non-HDL-C, HDL-C, TC, ApoB, LDL-C, and ApoA-1
- Adjudicated AP event rate in patients with two or more events of AP pancreatitis in 5 years prior to enrollment
- Adjudicated AP event rate
L2301: ## **Study Design**
L2302: This is a multi-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study done in conjunction with the TIMI Study Group, an academic research organization based at Brigham and Women's Hospital and Harvard Medical School. Eligible patients will enter an approximately 4-week screening period (that may be extended to 8 weeks if assessments cannot be completed within the 4-week period) that includes an at least 2-week diet/life-style stabilization/run-in period, and an approximately 2-week qualification period. Following qualification, approximately 152 eligible patients will be randomized 1:1 to Cohort A (50 mg) or Cohort B (80 mg) and each cohort further randomized 3:1 to receive olezarsen or placebo in a 53-week treatment period. Patients in Cohort A will receive 50 mg of olezarsen Q4W or matching volume of placebo (0.5 mL) during Weeks 1-49 of the treatment period. Patients in Cohort B will receive 80 mg olezarsen Q4W or matching volume of placebo (0.8 mL) during Weeks 1-49 of the treatment period. The 80 mg dose (blinded olezarsen or placebo) may be adjusted to 50 mg Q4W due to tolerability or safety reasons at any point during the study following consultation with the
L2303: Sponsor Medical Monitor or designee. Randomization will be stratified by qualification fasting TG levels of &lt;500 mg/dL vs. \ge500 mg/dL. Following the Week 53 visit, patients will enter the 13-week post-treatment follow-up period. All endpoints will be evaluated after the last patient has completed the Week 53/ET visit.
L2304: ## Population
L2305: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L2306: - Must have given written informed consent (signed and dated) and any authorizations required by local law and be able to comply with all study requirements
- Aged \ge18 years at the time of informed consent
- Fall into at least one of the following groups (a or b):
L2307: HTG with fasting TG \ge150 mg/dL (1.69 mmol/L) and &lt;500 mg/dL (5.65 mmol/L) with either
L2308: Clinical diagnosis of atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD) defined as documented coronary artery disease, cerebrovascular disease, or peripheral artery disease or
L2309: At increased risk for ASCVD defined as:
L2310: - Type 2 Diabetes mellitus, or
- Two (2) or more of the following CV risk enhancing factors:
- Men \ge55 years of age or women \ge65 years of age
- Women with a history of preeclampsia, or premature menopause (before the age of 40 years)
- Family history of premature ASCVD (before the age of 55 years for males, or before the age of 65 years for females)
- Current tobacco use (5 cigarettes/day or more for at least 1 year)
- Metabolic syndrome
- Hypertension requiring antihypertensive treatment
- Chronic kidney disease stage 3 or (eGFR 30-59 mL/min/m^{2})
- LDL-C \ge160 mg/dL
- LDL-C \ge100 mg/dL on therapy to lower LDL-C
- Elevated high-sensitivity C-reactive protein (\ge2.0 mg/L)
- Elevated lipoprotein (a) (\ge50 mg/dL or 125 nmol/L)
- Ankle brachial index &lt;0.9 in asymptomatic patients
- Coronary artery calcium score \ge100
L2311: sHTG with fasting TG \ge500 mg/dL (5.65 mmol/L)
L2312: - Patients should be on standard of care lipid-lowering medications per local guidelines. Lipid-lowering medications should be optimized and stabilized for at least 4 weeks prior to screening to minimize changes in these medications during the study. Patients taking OTC omega-3 fatty acids should make every effort to remain on the same brand through the end of the study.
L2313: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2314: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria**
L2315: - Diabetes with any of the following:Newly diagnosed within 12 weeks of screening
- HbA1c \ge 9.5\% at screening
- Change in basal insulin regimen &gt;20\% within 3 months prior to screening
- For patients with type 1 diabetes: episode of diabetic ketoacidosis, or \ge 3 episodes of severe hypoglycemia within 6 months prior to screening
- Acute coronary syndrome or stroke/ transient ischemic attack within 6 months prior to screening
- Major surgery, peripheral revascularization, or non-urgent percutaneous coronary intervention within 3 months prior to screening, or upcoming planned major surgery or major procedure (e.g., arterial revascularization) during the course of the study
- Active pancreatitis within 4 weeks prior to screening
L2316: ## **Sample Size**
L2317: Approximately 152 subjects
L2318: ## **Treatments**
L2319: - Cohort A: Subjects will be randomized 3:1 to receive 50 mg olezarsen or matching volume of placebo SC Q4W for Weeks 1-49. Administered SC 0.5 mL volume
- Cohort B: Subjects will be randomized 3:1 to receive 80 mg olezarsen or matching volume of placebo SC Q4W for Weeks 1-49. Administered SC 0.8 mL volume
L2320: ## **Endpoints**
L2321: ## **Primary**
L2322: - Percent change in fasting TG from baseline at Month 6 (average of Weeks 25 and 27) compared to placebo
L2323: ## **Secondary**
L2324: - Percent change in fasting TG from baseline at Month 12 (average of Weeks 51 and 53) compared to placebo
- Proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;150 mg/dL (1.69 mmol/L) at Month 6 and proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;150 mg/dL at Month 12 compared to placebo in the subgroup of patients with baseline TG &lt;500 mg/dL (5.65 mmol/L)
- Proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;500 mg/dL (5.65 mmol/L) at Month 6 and proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;500 mg/dL at Month 12 compared to placebo in the subgroup of patients with baseline TG \ge 500 mg/dL
- Percent change in fasting ApoC-III, VLDL-C, remnant cholesterol, non-HDL-C, HDL-C, TC, ApoB, LDL-C, and ApoA-1 from baseline at Months 6 and 12 compared to placebo

- Adjudicated AP event rate during the treatment period compared to placebo

L2338: ## Statistical Plan
L2339: ## **Sample Size Considerations**
L2340: With 33 patients in each olezarsen treatment group and 22 in the pooled placebo group, there would be an approximately 80% power to detect a 60% difference between each olezarsen treatment group and pooled placebo group at an alpha level of 0.05 (two-sided), assuming 60% reduction in the olezarsen treatment group, no change in the placebo patients, and a common SD of approximately 73%. It is estimated that drop-out rate is approximately 20%. Approximately 152 patients will be enrolled in this trial to account for potential early dropouts and to facilitate general safety evaluations.
L2341: ## **Analysis of the Primary Endpoint**
L2342: The primary analysis of the primary endpoint is to compare the percent CFB to Month 6 in fasting TG between each olezarsen treatment group vs. pooled placebo group using an ANCOVA model in the FAS. The ANCOVA model will include the effects of treatment (olezarsen 80 mg, olezarsen 50 mg, or placebo), and log-transformed baseline TG. If patients have intercurrent event(s) before Week 25, e.g., treatment discontinuation, use of additional medication, changes in background or concomitant treatments, the treatment policy strategy will
L2343: be implemented, the patients' assessments post intercurrent events will continue to be collected. The missing TG value at Months 6 or 12 will be imputed using an imputation model that contains the following variables: baseline fasting TG, fasting TG at each postbaseline visit and the multiple imputation will be stratified by treatment group (Schafer 1997; Schafer 1999).
L2344: The Markov chain Monte Carlo method will be used under the multivariate normality assumption to impute the missing values by treatment group. To conform to the multivariate normality assumption, baseline and postbaseline TG will be natural log-transformed before the imputation process, the data will be back transformed to create the imputed data set after the imputation.
L2345: The primary efficacy analysis will take place after the last patient has completed the Week 53/ET visit and the database has been locked. Details of the controlled access to the unblinded data will be outlined in the SAP and unblinding plan.
L2346: The following sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoint will be conducted to assess the robustness of the primary analysis result, and details of the analyses will be outlined in the SAP:
L2347: The primary analysis described above will be repeated in the PPS.
L2348: - The primary analysis will be repeated in the subset of FAS with no missing TG value at baseline and Month 6.
- A nonparametric Wilcoxon rank-sum test will be performed on the percent change in TG from baseline to Month 6. Missing data will be handled using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis. The treatment difference will be assessed using the Hodges-Lehmann estimator of the location shift and corresponding 95% CI. The analysis will be conducted in the FAS.
- A two-dimensional tipping-point analysis will be conducted on the imputed data (based on the primary imputation approach as described above) to assess the influence of missingness on the conclusion of the treatment difference. A range of penalties will be added to the imputed values for each group until the result becomes non-significant and tipping points are identified. The analysis will be conducted in the FAS.
- A few sensitivity analyses using controlled imputations (pattern mixture models [PMM]) with ANCOVA similar to that for primary analysis will be conducted in the FAS to assess the robustness of the primary analysis results. With these approaches, missing not at random is assumed and the mean function for the missing data from patients treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study is pre-specified in ways that assess the robustness of the missing at random assumption in the primary analysis.Patients with missing TG value at Month 6 will be imputed using a PMM. Specifically, for patients treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study due to AE or lack of efficacy (i.e., informative missing), their missing TG data after treatment discontinuation will be multiply imputed using the CIR approach based on the estimates from of the placebo patients. The CIR approach is detailed in (Carpenter et al. 2013). The assumption is when a patient discontinues treatment due to informative missing, from discontinuation onwards he/she would progress in the same way as the patients in the placebo group. For patients who discontinue due to other reasons and all patients from the placebo group, their missing data will be treated as non-informative missing, and data will be multiply
L2349: imputed based on the distribution of their own arm. The analysis will be conducted in the FAS. Baseline and postbaseline TG will be natural log-transformed before the imputation process, the data will be back transformed to create the imputed data set after the imputation.

- All patients treated with olezarsen who discontinue the study will have missing post-treatment discontinuation TG values multiply imputed using the CIR approach based on the estimates from the placebo patients. Missing TG for placebo patients will be imputed using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis
- Patients with missing TG values after treatment discontinuation will have TG values multiply imputed based on the known assessments from the retrieved dropouts (e.g., for Month 6, the retrieved dropouts include those patients who discontinue treatment before Week 25 but still have assessments at Week 25 or 27), if there are a sufficient number of retrieved dropouts. The imputation model will include the following variables: fasting TG values at baseline and postbaseline visits and the multiple imputation will be stratified by treatment group. If there is no sufficient number of retrieved dropouts, missing TG value after treatment discontinuation at Month 6 or 12 will be imputed using a “wash-out” (Jump to Reference, J2R) multiple imputation approach. With the approach, patients treated with olezarsen who discontinue study will have missing post-discontinuation TG values multiply imputed based on baseline fasting TG and history of pancreatitis (yes/no) of the patient, and the imputation model for the placebo group. Patients in the placebo group who discontinue the study will have missing post-treatment discontinuation TG values multiply imputed using the myocardial infarction method as the primary analysis. Baseline and postbaseline TG will be natural log-transformed before the imputation process, the data will be back transformed to create the imputed data set after the imputation.
L2350: ## Analysis of Secondary Endpoints
L2351: - The percent change in fasting TG from baseline to Month 12 in the FAS will be analyzed in the same way as for the primary endpoint.
- The proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;150 mg/dL (1.69 mmol/L) at Month 6 and proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;150 mg/dL at Month 12 in the subset of FAS with baseline TG &lt;500 mg/dL will be compared between each olezarsen treatment group and pooled placebo group using a logistic regression model with the treatment group as the factor, and log-transformed baseline TG as a covariate. Missing TG values will be imputed using the same multiple imputation method as the primary analysis of the primary endpoint. The analysis will be repeated in the subset of PPS with baseline TG &lt;500 mg/dL as a sensitivity analysis. An additional sensitivity analysis will be conducted in the subset of FAS with baseline TG &lt;500 mg/dL, in which all patients who have missing data due to discontinuation will be considered as non-responders.
- The proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;500 mg/dL at Month 6 and proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;500 mg/dL at Month 12 will be analyzed in the same way
L2352: ## NDA 218614
## Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2353: as the proportion of patients who achieve fasting TG &lt;150 mg/dL. It will be conducted in the subset of FAS or PPS with baseline TG \ge 500 mg/dL.
L2354: - The percent change in fasting ApoC-III, VLDL-C, remnant cholesterol, non-HDL-C, HDL-C, TC, ApoB, LDL-C, and ApoA-1 from baseline to Months 6 and 12 in the FAS will be analyzed in the same way as for the primary endpoint.
- The adjudicated AP event rates in the subset of FAS with two or more events of adjudicated AP in 5 years prior to enrollment and in the FAS during the treatment period (Week 1 through Week 53/ET) will be compared between pooled olezarsen treatment and placebo group using a negative binomial regression model or Poisson regression model with the treatment group and log transformed baseline TG as the factors, and number of adjudicated AP events in 5 years prior to the enrollment as a covariate. The logarithm of time in year that each patient observed during the treatment period will be used as an offset variable. In the case of not enough events to perform the model-based analysis, the number and percentage of subjects along with the two-sided 95% exact CI using Clopper-Pearson methodology will be summarized based on FAS. Fisher's exact p-value will also be provided.
L2355: ## 15.3. Trial ISIS 678354-CS13
L2356: The following is the Applicant's protocol synopsis of Trial ISIS 678354-CS13 from protocol amendment 4 (March 3, 2023), which was the final version of the protocol prior to the data cutoff.
L2357: ## Title
L2358: An Open-Label Extension Study of AKCEA-APOCIII-LRX Administered Subcutaneously to Patients With Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome (FCS)
L2359: ## Landmark Dates
L2360: - First subject enrolled and dosed: December 20, 2021
- Last Subject, Last Visit: Not applicable, study ongoing
- Data Cutoff Date: August 5, 2023 (interim)
- Database Lock Date: September 21, 2023 (interim)
L2361: ## Objectives
L2362: ## Primary
L2363: - To evaluate the safety and tolerability of olezarsen
L2364: ## Study Design
L2365: This is a multi-center OLE study of FCS patients rolling-over from ISIS 678354-CS3 (the index study). Patient eligibility should be determined during the Qualification Period within 31 days prior to study entry. Patients will receive olezarsen at the dose of 80 mg Q4W (or 50 mg every-4-weeks if their dose was permanently reduced from 80 mg to 50 mg (olezarsen or placebo) in the index study) during Weeks 1 through 153 of a 157-week Treatment Period, followed by a 13-week post-treatment follow-up period. The 80 mg dose may be adjusted to 50 mg Q4W
L2366: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2367: (temporarily or permanently) for tolerability or safety reasons following consultation with the Sponsor Medical Monitor. Dietary counseling will be reinforced at intervals throughout the treatment and post-treatment follow-up periods.
L2368: ## **Population**
L2369: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L2370: - Satisfactory completion of the ISIS 678354-CS3 index study (last dose as scheduled at Week 49) with an acceptable safety profile, per investigator judgement.
L2371: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria**
L2372: - Have any new condition or worsening of existing condition which in the opinion of the investigator would make the patient unsuitable for enrollment, or could interfere with the patient participating in or completing the study, including need for treatment with medications disallowed in the index study (ISIS 678354-CS3).
L2373: ## **Treatments**
L2374: For each individual patient who receives 80 mg of olezarsen, it will be administered SC either as a single 0.8 mL injection using vial, or 80 mg autoinjector every-4-weeks for a total of 39 doses (Weeks 1 to 153). For each individual patient who has dose reduced to 50 mg of olezarsen, it will be administered SC either as a single 0.5 mL injection using vial, or using a 50 mg autoinjector every-4-weeks.
L2375: ## **Endpoints**
L2376: Safety endpoints include a proportion of patients who show the following changes from baseline to Week 53, Week 105, and Week 157:*
L2377: - Decrease in platelet count by &gt;30%
- Decrease in platelet count by &gt;50%
- Platelet count value &lt;50,000 mm3
- Major bleeding events
- Clinically relevant non-major bleeding events
- Decrease in eGFR by &gt;30%
- Decrease in eGFR by &gt;50%
- UPCR \ge1000 mg/g
- UACR \ge500 mg/g
- ALT or AST &gt;5 \times ULN
- Total bilirubin &gt;2.0 mg/dL
- ALT or AST &gt;3 \times ULN and total bilirubin &gt;2 \times ULN
L2378: * *Lab values based on confirmed results.*
L2379: ## NDA 218614
## Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2380: In addition, rate of MACE will be summarized. Tolerability assessments include AEs, clinical laboratory tests, and use of concomitant medications.
L2381: Other endpoints included changes in lipid parameters such as TG and ApoB48 (average changes and categorical changes) and rate of pancreatitis events.

L2432: ## 15.4. Trial ISIS 678354-CS7
L2433: The following is the Applicant's protocol synopsis of Trial ISIS 678354-CS7 from protocol amendment 3 (February 9, 2023), which was the final version of the protocol prior to the data cutoff.
L2434: ## Title
L2435: An Open-Label Safety Study of AKCEA-APOCIII-LRX Administered Subcutaneously to Patients With Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome (FCS) Previously Treated With Volanesorsen (ISIS 304801)
L2436: ## Landmark Dates
L2437: - First Subject Enrolled: March 22, 2022
- Last Subject Enrolled: February 28, 2023
- First Subject Dosed: March 24, 2022
- Last Subject, Last Visit: Ongoing
- Data Cutoff Date: May 31, 2023 (interim)
- Database Lock Date: July 31, 2023 (interim)
L2438: ## Objectives
L2439: ## **Primary**
L2440: To evaluate the safety and tolerability of olezarsen in subjects with FCS previously treated with volanesorsen
L2441: ## **Secondary**
L2442: To evaluate the PK and PD effects of olezarsen in subjects with FCS previously treated with volanesorsen
L2443: ## Study Design
L2444: This is a multi-center open-label safety study of FCS patients previously treated with volanesorsen (ISIS 304801) to guide the transition to olezarsen.
L2445: Following an up to 8-week screening period (including an up to 4-week run-in washout period for patients treated with volanesorsen within 4 weeks prior to screening, and a 4-week, but not
L2446: ## NDA 218614
## Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2447: less than, 2-week qualification period), patients (N= ~24) will receive olezarsen at least 6 weeks after their last dose of volanesorsen at the dose of 80 mg Q4W during Weeks 1 to 153 of the 157-week treatment period, followed by a 13-week post-treatment follow-up period. This dose may be adjusted to 50 mg Q4W due to tolerability or safety reasons at any point during the study following consultation with the Sponsor Medical Monitor.
L2448: Dietary counseling will be reinforced at intervals throughout the treatment and post-treatment evaluation periods.
L2449: An independent Data Safety Monitoring Board will periodically review the data collected.
L2450: An independent Adjudication Committee will adjudicate all AP events on study.
L2451: ## **Population**
L2452: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L2453: - Patients with FCS (clinical or genetic diagnosis) currently on or previously treated with volanesorsen (ISIS 304801)
L2454: Study participants in countries where Waylivra® is commercially approved and available for the patients should not be deprived of the treatment option with Waylivra®. Participation in this study for such patients will only be allowed when Waylivra® was discontinued due to AEs.
L2455: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria**
L2456: - Treatment with another investigational drug (non-oligonucleotide), biological agent, or device within 4 weeks of screening, or 5 half-lives of investigational agent, whichever is longer
- Have any other conditions including significant medical history which, in the opinion of the investigator would make the patient unsuitable for inclusion, or could interfere with the patient participating in or completing the study
L2457: ## **Treatments**
L2458: For each individual patient who receives 80 mg of olezarsen, it will be administered SC either as a single 0.8 mL injection using vial, or 80 mg autoinjector, Q4W for a total of 39 doses (Week 1 to 153).
L2459: For each individual patient who has dose reduced to 50 mg of olezarsen it will be administered SC either as a single 0.5 mL injection using vial, or a single (b)/(4) mL injection using a 50 mg autoinjector, Q4W
L2460: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2461: ## **Endpoints**
L2462: Safety endpoints include a proportion of patients who show the following changes from baseline to Week 53, Week 105, and Week 157:*
L2463: - Decrease in platelet count by &gt;30%
- Decrease in platelet count by &gt;50%
- Platelet count value &lt;50,000 mm3
- Major bleeding events
- Clinically relevant non-major bleeding events
- Decrease in eGFR by &gt;30%
- Decrease in eGFR by &gt;50%
- UPCR \ge1000 mg/g
- UACR \ge500 mg/g
- ALT or AST &gt;5 \times ULN
- Total bilirubin &gt;2.0 mg/dL
- ALT or AST &gt;3 \times ULN and total bilirubin &gt;2 \times ULN
L2464: * *Lab values based on confirmed results.*
L2465: Tolerability assessments include AEs clinical laboratory tests, and use of concomitant medications.
L2466: Other endpoints included changes in lipid parameters such as TG and ApoB48 (average changes and categorical changes) and rate of pancreatitis events.

L2499: ## 16.1. Trial ISIS 678354-CS3, Subgroup Analyses
L2500: ## Subgroup Analyses of the Primary Endpoint for Study CS3
L2501: Subgroup analyses were performed on the primary endpoint, fasting TG by age group (&lt;65 years, ≥65 years), sex (female, male), region (outside of USA, USA), race (Asian, Native Hawaiian or Other Pacific Islander, Other, White), and ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, Not Hispanic or Latino). Figure 67 shows the subgroup analysis results for olezarsen 50 mg vs. placebo, and Figure 68 shows the subgroup analysis results for olezarsen 80 mg vs. placebo. The subgroup analyses were performed using the FAS population. This study was conducted in mainly White, females, &lt;65 years of age, outside of the USA. Due to some very small patient subgroups, some estimates/95% CIs were not able to be calculated. The subgroup for race was combined to Other and White due to very few patients in the Asian, Native Hawaiian and Other Pacific Islander, and Other categories.
L2502: Figure 67. Subgroup Analysis: Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL) at Month 6, Olezarsen 50 mg vs. Placebo, Trial CS3
L2503: Source: Statistical Reviewer's Analysis; adsl.xpt, adeff.xpt
L2504: The primary analysis time point (Month 6) was defined as the average of Weeks 23, 25, and 27.
L2505: Note: LS Means are from the analysis of covariance model with treatment (Olezarsen 50 mg, Placebo), the two randomization stratification factors, prior history of pancreatitis within 10 years prior to Screening (yes vs. no), previous treatment with volanesorsen (yes vs. no) as the fixed effects and log-transformed Baseline triglyceride as a covariate for each subgroup. Missing data was imputed using Washout imputation.
L2506: Other: Asian, Native Hawaiian and Other Pacific Islander, and Other
L2507: Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; Ole, number of subjects in Olezarsen group; Placebo, number of subjects in Placebo group; LS Means, least squares means; LCL, lower confidence; UCL, upper confidence
L2508: **Figure 68. Subgroup Analysis: Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL) at Month 6, Olezarsen 80 mg vs. Placebo, Trial CS3**
L2509: Source: Statistical Reviewer's Analysis; adsl.xpt, adef.xpt
The primary analysis time point (Month 6) was defined as the average of Weeks 23, 25, and 27.
Note: LS Means are from the analysis of covariance model with treatment (Olezarsen 50 mg, Placebo), the two randomization stratification factors, prior history of pancreatitis within 10 years prior to Screening (yes vs. no), previous treatment with volanesorsen (yes vs. no) as the fixed effects and log-transformed Baseline triglyceride as a covariate for each subgroup. Missing data was imputed using Washout imputation.
Other: Asian, Native Hawaiian and Other Pacific Islander, and Other
Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; LCL, lower confidence; LS Means, least squares means; Ole, number of subjects in Olezarsen group; Placebo, number of subjects in Placebo group; UCL, upper confidence
L2510: There were likely some random highs and random lows in sample estimates of subgroup treatment effects due to small sample size and large variability for some subgroups. Therefore, we also derived shrinkage estimates of subgroup treatment effects using a Bayesian hierarchical model based on summary sample estimates. The total variability in the sample estimates is the sum of the within subgroup variability of the sample estimator and the across subgroups variability in underlying/true parameter values. A shrinkage estimate of the subgroup treatment effect, which borrows information from the other subgroups while estimating the treatment effect for a specific subgroup, is a “weighted” average of the sample estimate and overall estimate. The weights are based on the ratio of the between subgroup variability to the within subgroup variability. The greater that ratio the smaller the weight on the overall estimate (the less the shrinkage). We used the same flat prior to derive shrinkage estimates for all subgroups (age, sex,
L2511: region, race, and ethnicity). The Bayesian hierarchical model assumptions for fasting TG at Month 6 are:
L2512: For i = 1, 2, \dots Y_i represents the observed sample estimate of treatment effect in a subgroup level i, assume Y_i \sim N(\mu_i, \sigma_i^2) where
L2513: - \sigma_i^2 are the observed variance for sample estimates
- \mu_i \sim N(\mu, \tau^2)
- \mu \sim N(0, 140), 1/\tau^2 \sim \text{Gamma}(0.001, 0.001)
L2514: The variance should be selected relative to the residual standard deviation from the primary analysis model. In general, it should be 4 to 10 times of the residual SD. A flat prior with mean 0 and SD of 140 was chosen. This SD is 4 times the residual SD and so this assumption would not be influential. Figure 69 for olezarsen 50 mg vs. placebo and Figure 70 for olezarsen 80 mg vs. placebo compares the conventional subgroup analysis results of the sample estimate (in blue) and Bayesian shrinkage estimate (in red) for the endpoint of fasting TG at Month 6. The overall treatment effect for 50 mg was -21.5 (95% CI: -48.2, 5.3) and for 80 mg was -44.5 (95% CI: -72.0, -17.0). Subgroup analysis using Bayesian shrinkage estimate exhibits narrower CI, and the shrinkage subgroup estimate is closer to the overall mean.
L2515: **Figure 69. Subgroup Shrinkage Analysis: Mean Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL) at Month 6 Olezarsen 50 mg vs. Placebo, Trial CS3**
L2516: Source: Statistical Reviewer's Analysis; adsl.xpt, adeff.xpt; Other:
L2517: **Figure 70. Subgroup Shrinkage Analysis: Mean Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL) at Month 6 Olezarsen 80 mg vs. Placebo, Trial CS3**
L2518: Source: Statistical Reviewer's Analysis; adsl.xpt, adeff.xpt
L2519: ## 16.2. Trial ISIS 678354-CS3, Supportive Analyses
L2520: Additional supportive evidence of TG reduction observed with olezarsen 80 mg as compared to placebo are shown in the following tables and figures in several lipid and lipoprotein parameters.
L2521: Figure 71 shows the mean (95% CI) change from baseline in TG over time. This figure demonstrates that the reduction in TG with olezarsen 80 mg showed persistence over time until the end of the trial.
L2522: NDA 218614
L2523: Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2524: Figure 71. Mean (95% CI) Change From Baseline Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL), Trial CS3
L2525: Source: Clinical Reviewer Analysis, JMP Clinical
L2526: Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval
L2527: Figure 72 shows a waterfall plot of the mean change from baseline in fasting TG levels for the treatment arms in Trial CS3 over the 53-week treatment period.
L2528: NDA 218614
L2529: Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2530: Figure 72. Mean Change From Baseline in Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL), Trial CS3
L2531: Source: Clinical Reviewer Analysis, JMP Clinical
L2532: The TG waterfall plot displays each individual subject's TG level change and is a visual representation of the effectiveness of olezarsen 80 mg treatment.
L2533: Figure 73 shows the mean change from baseline in LDL-C as a waterfall plot of all the subjects in Trial CS3.
L2534: Figure 73. Mean Change From Baseline in Fasting LDL-C (mg/dL), Trial CS3
L2535: Source: Clinical Reviewer Analysis, JMP Clinical
Abbreviations: LDL-C, low-density lipoprotein cholesterol
L2536: As shown in Figure 73 above, LDL-C increased in subjects on olezarsen treatment as compared to subjects on placebo. An increase in LDL-C following TG-lowering therapy often occurs because treatments that reduce TG tend to reduce VLDL particles, which are rich in TG. As VLDL is metabolized, some of it is converted into LDL particles, thus increasing the overall amount of LDL-C in circulation. Because subjects with FCS have very low levels of LDL-C, the increase in LDL-C more often remains within the normal range for LDL-C levels. However, clinicians will need to evaluate and monitor subjects' LDL-C levels, especially those at higher cardiovascular risk.
L2537: Figure 74 shows the mean change in non-HDL-C as a waterfall plot for all subjects in Trial CS3 over the 53-week treatment period.
L2538: Figure 74. Mean Change From Baseline in Fasting Non-HDL-C (mg/dL), Trial CS3
L2539: Source: Clinical Reviewer Analysis, JMP Clinical
Abbreviations: HDL-C, high-density lipoprotein cholesterol
L2540: Non-HDL-C is a measure of all the cholesterol carried by lipoproteins other than HDL. Non-HDL-C includes LDL-C, VLDL-C, and intermediate-density lipoprotein cholesterol. As shown in Figure 74 above, non-HDL-C levels decreased with olezarsen 80 mg to a greater degree than placebo. Although non-HDL-C does not measure TG directly, high levels of TG often correlate with elevated VLDL cholesterol, and lower levels of TG correlate with lower levels of VLDL-C, which is part of the non-HDL-C value.
L2541: The following waterfall plot depicts mean CFB in total ApoB.
L2542: Figure 75. Mean Change From Baseline Fasting Total ApoB (mg/dL), Trial CS3
L2543: Source: Clinical Reviewer Analysis, JMP Clinical
Abbreviations: ApoB, apolipoprotein B
L2544: Total ApoB is a measure of the total number of atherogenic lipoproteins in the blood. ApoB is a protein found on the surface of certain lipoproteins, including LDL-C, VLDL-C, intermediate-density lipoprotein and lipoprotein(a). The increase in total ApoB may reflect the increase in LDL-C seen with olezarsen treatment.
L2545: Figure 76 depicts a waterfall plot of the mean change from baseline in HDL-C for Trial CS3.
L2546: Figure 76. Mean Change From Baseline Fasting HDL-C (mg/dL), Trial CS3
L2547: Source: Clinical Reviewer Analysis, JMP Clinical
Abbreviations: HDL-C, high-density lipoprotein cholesterol
L2548: HDL-C helps transport cholesterol levels from the bloodstream and tissues back to the liver, where it can be processed and removed from the body. In observational studies, higher levels of HDL-C are associated with a lower risk of heart disease. However, human genetics and failed clinical trials have created uncertainties about targeting HDL-C to reduce CV risk (Rader and Hovingh 2014). As shown in Figure 76 above, treatment with olezarsen (both doses) increased levels of HDL-C as compared to placebo. However, the clinical implications of the increases in HDL-C in the FCS population are unknown.
L2549: ## 16.3. Trial ISIS 678354-CS3, Clinical Outcome Assessment
L2550: This section briefly reviews the PROs and COAs for the evaluation of clinical benefit. Refer to the full Division of Clinical Outcome Assessment review for a detailed review.
L2551: ## **Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome Symptoms and Impacts Scale**
L2552: The FCS-SIS is a 17-item PRO instrument designed to assess FCS symptoms and associated impacts. It consists of two domains: symptom domain (4 items) and impact domain (13 items). Items in the symptom domain are rated on an 11-point numeric rating scale ranging from 0 (“No [symptom]”) to 10 (“worst possible [symptom]”). Items in the impact domain are rated on a verbal rating scale ranging from “Never” to “Always.” The recall period for the symptom
L2553: ## NDA 218614
## Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2554: domain is the previous two weeks.9 The recall period for the impact domain is momentary (“currently”). The FCS symptom domain (2-week recall) was administered at baseline, thereafter the 24-hour recall version was administered daily (minimum of 4 daily assessments per week) at baseline to Week 17. The FCS impacts scale was administered at screening, baseline, and then Q4W thereafter. See Figure 77 for a copy of the instrument (24-hour recall version and impacts scales).
L2555: 9 There are two versions of the Symptoms Domain, one with a recall period of “past 24 hours” (referred to as FCS Symptom) and another with a 2-Week recall period (referred to as FCS Symptoms Two Week Recall). Note the two week recall version was administered at screening and the 24-hour recall version was administered at baseline and postbaseline visits.
L2556: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2557: **Figure 77. FCS-SIS (24-Hour Recall Version and Impacts Scales)**
L2558: (b) (4)
L2559: (Figure 77 continued)
L2560: (b) (4)
L2561: Source: ISIS 678354-CS3 Amendment 9 dated August 2, 2023, Appendix E.
Abbreviations: FCS, familial chylomicronemia syndrome; FCS-SIS, Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome Symptoms and Impacts Scale
L2562: ## **Review of COAs for Evaluation of Clinical Benefit**
L2563: The Applicant did not submit an evidence dossier for any of the COAs and/or evidence to assess the fitness-for-purpose of the measures utilized in Trial CS3. Therefore, these instruments could not be reviewed for content validity, other measurement properties, and clinically meaningful change. In the absence of evidence, the FDA cannot make a definitive conclusion as to whether the COAs can support regulatory decision-making.
L2564: The Applicant conducted an exit interview substudy to provide further support for the content validity of the FCS-SIS and to facilitate the interpretation of the olezarsen clinical trial results. While the Applicant generated qualitative data to interpret olezarsen clinical trial results, the data are difficult to interpret due to limitations of the substudy (i.e., exit interviews conducted in the OLE phase of Trial CS3).
L2565: The data from Trial CS3 demonstrated that for olezarsen (80 mg and 50 mg) there was no change from baseline differences in any of the PRO scores compared with placebo. However, the data
L2566: from the exit interview substudy indicated that a total of 14 of the 18 patients (78%) reported improvement in at least one of their pre-trial symptoms and impacts and most improvements were considered meaningful.
L2567: In the absence of evidence, the Applicant has not demonstrated that any of the COAs are fit-for-purpose in the specified context of use. Further, the findings from Trial CS3 and exit interview substudy were not conclusive with improvements in FCS symptoms and impacts (median change was generally 0 in the FCS-SIS measure). As such, it is difficult to determine whether any treatment benefits were observed as they relate to FCS symptoms and impacts.
L2568: Refer to the full Division of Clinical Outcome Assessment review for a detailed review of the COAs and exit interview substudy.

L2604: ## Supplementary Safety Review—Changes in Other Hematopoietic Parameters
L2605: ## Issue
L2606: Higher proportions of olezarsen 80 mg treated subjects compared to placebo-treated subjects reported an event in CS3 using the FMQ (narrow) search terms ‘anemia’ and ‘leukopenia’ and ‘anemia’ (both 50 mg and 80 mg olezarsen compared to placebo) in CS8.
L2607: ## Background
L2608: Because of the reduction in platelet count observed with olezarsen treatment, there was concern that other hematopoietic parameters may be adversely affected.
L2609: ## Assessment
L2610: Higher proportions of olezarsen 80 mg treated subjects compared to placebo-treated subjects reported an event in CS3 using the FMQ (narrow) search terms ‘anemia’ and ‘leukopenia’ and ‘anemia’ (both 50 mg and 80 mg olezarsen compared to placebo) in CS8.

L2645: In CS3, adverse shifts in Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event toxicity grades of \ge 2 severity categories in hemoglobin occurred in three olezarsen-treated subjects and no placebo-treated subjects10. One of the three olezarsen-treated subjects (CS3-(b) (6)) experienced a shift in hemoglobin from within reference range to a ‘severe’ category. This subject is described below:
L2646: - Subject CS3-(b) (6) was treated with olezarsen 50 mg, experienced a reduction in hemoglobin from 12.3 g/dL (within reference range) at baseline to a nadir of 7.7 g/dL (severe) on Study Day 150. The subject had voluntarily withdrawn from the Trial CS3 on Study Day 114 (last dose of olezarsen Study Day 90) – she had recently been hospitalized for an episode of pancreatitis (Study Day 102). On the same day as the low hemoglobin (Study Day 150), the subject presented to the emergency department with chest pain. During the examination her heart rate was registered at 180 bpm. No abnormal troponin was reported. An ECG was performed which showed Atrioventricular Nodal Reentrant Tachycardia. She received 5 mg IV of adenosine. Her heart rate returned to sinus rhythm, and she was discharged. The subject’s hemoglobin improved at the next study visit (also last visit – follow-up week 13, Study Day 189) to 9.9 g/dL (moderate) and ECG was normal.

L2656: Significant reductions in white blood cells, lymphocytes, and neutrophils may lead to increased susceptibility to infections and infestations. There were no significant differences across treatment groups in the SOC of 'Infections and infestations' in Trials CS3 or CS8.
L2657: ## Conclusion
L2658: Small imbalances were noted with olezarsen treatment for AEs related to anemia and when evaluating categorical changes in WBC, lymphocytes, and neutrophils in Trials CS3 and CS8. No significant differences were noted in mean CFB in hemoglobin and WBC over time in Trials CS3 and CS8. No clinically meaningful consequences were observed in that there were no significant differences across treatment groups for evaluation of infections in CS3 or CS8.
L2659: ## Supplementary Safety Review–Renal Function
L2660: ## Background
L2661: Renal toxicity has been observed after administration of volanesorsen and other SC and IV administered antisense oligonucleotides (Akcea Therapeutics 2024b).

L2679: ## Supplementary Information for Key Safety Review Issue–Immunogenicity
L2680: Subjects with a positive ADA status could be further classified into different ADA types based on their baseline ADA status and change in ADA titer post-treatment. These definitions are described in Section 17.
L2681: ## Treatment-Emergent ADA
L2682: Subjects with either treatment-induced ADA or treatment-boosted ADA as described below:
L2683: - Treatment-induced ADA: ADA developed de novo (seroconversion) following study drug administration (i.e., formation of ADA any time after the initial drug administration in a subject without pre-existing ADA, i.e., baseline negative ADA).
- Treatment-boosted ADA: pre-existing ADA that were boosted to a higher level following study drug administration (i.e., any time after the initial drug administration the ADA titer is greater than the baseline titer by a factor of 8-fold or more).
L2684: ## Treatment-Unaffected
L2685: ADA: pre-existing ADA that were not affected (boosted) following study drug administration (i.e., any time after the initial drug administration the ADA titer is 4-fold or less).
L2686: Subjects with positive ADA status that was treatment-induced were also classified as being ‘transient’ or ‘persistent’ based on the following definitions.
L2687: Transient ADA response:
L2688: - Treatment-induced ADA detected only at one sampling time point during the treatment or follow-up observation period (excluding the last sampling time point, which will be considered persistent unless shown to be undetectable at a later time) or
- Treatment-induced ADA detected at two or more sampling time points during the treatment (including follow-up period if any), where the first and last ADA-positive samples (irrespective of any negative samples in between) are separated by a period less than 16 weeks, and the subject’s last sampling time point is ADA-negative.
L2689: Persistent ADA response:
L2690: - Treatment-induced ADA detected at two or more sampling time points during the treatment (including follow-up period if any), where the first and last ADA-positive samples
L2691: ## NDA 218614
## Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2692: (irrespective of any negative samples in between) are separated by a period of 16 weeks or longer or
L2693: - Treatment-induced ADA detected only at the last sampling time point of the study treatment period or at a sampling time point with less than 16 weeks before an ADA negative last sample.

L2697: Positive: Patients have at least 1 confirmed positive sample result at any time during the treatment or post-treatment evaluation periods, which include the following:
L2698: Treatment-Induced ADA: ADA developed de novo (seroconversion) following study drug administration (i.e., formation of ADA any time after the initial drug administration in a patient without pre-existing ADA, i.e., Baseline negative ADA).

L2703: ## Pregnancy
L2704: There was one paternal exposure during pregnancy reported. Subject CS3-(b) (6), a 43-year-old man enrolled in Trial CS3 and randomized to olezarsen 80 mg (first dose on (b) (6)). On Study Day 8, the subject reported paternal exposure during pregnancy. The subject's partner was a 27-year-old, with first date of last menstrual period of (b) (6), with expected delivery (b) (6). The gestational age at the time of drug exposure was estimated to be week 5. Infant was born (b) (6), via elective caesarian delivery. On (b) (6) (Study Day 219), the male infant experienced supra and infra tentorial morphological changes, and changes in the eyeballs/papilla and optic nerve that were suggestive of tubulinopathy. Per the infant's pediatrician, the diagnosis could be West Syndrome, but this was not confirmed.
L2705: On (b) (6) (Study Day 437), the infant, who was approximately 8 months old at this time, was hospitalized for muscle spasms. Treatment with anti-convulsive medication was initiated which stopped the involuntary movements. The infant was improving with physiotherapy to strengthen his muscles but was still unable to sit up.
L2706: NDA 218614
Tryngolza (olezarsen sodium)
L2707: ## **Reviewer Comment**
L2708: Discussion with the non-clinical review team regarding this case noted that in the fertility studies of both olezarsen and volanesorsen, drug treated males were mated with drug-treated females, so sex specificity of effects cannot be ascertained. However, there were no effects on fetal development reported with olezarsen. For volanesorsen, there were increases in rib variations (not statistically significant) noted in the offspring, but nothing analogous to the muscle spasms or eye issues reported clinically. In the pre-/postnatal toxicity study with volanesorsen in which pregnant females were treated throughout gestation and lactation, there were reductions in maternal body weights which resulted in lower pup weights at the HD of 25 mg/kg (approximately 25x the clinical dose based on BSA). The reduced pup weights in turn contributed to slight delays in the occurrence of various developmental landmarks. Delays of this nature are not unusual when there are effects on pup body weights.
L2709: Tissue distribution studies provide further support for the effect not being related to olezarsen. In the rat, testicular tissue levels averaged 0.2% of a single SC dose of 5 mg/kg (approximately 3-fold the clinical dose on a BSA basis). Given the low level in testicular tissue, targeted delivery to the liver if any drug in the ejaculate actually made it into the female circulatory system and across the placental barrier, it is improbable that treatment of the male partner led to the observed anomalies.
L2710: There are anecdotal case reports in the literature of women with FCS treated with volanesorsen during their pregnancies with successful outcomes for both mother and baby (Wanninayake et al. 2024).
L2711: ## **Autoinjector**
L2712: The to-be-marketed formulation of olezarsen will be an autoinjector. The autoinjector was introduced into the open-label clinical trials, CS7 and CS13. In CS13, at the time of the NDA submission there were 32 subjects administering olezarsen with the autoinjector with a mean exposure of 99 days (minimum 6 days, maximum 199 days). At the 90-day safety update, there were 56 subjects with a mean exposure of 151 days (minimum 28 days, maximum 317 days).
L2713: Use of the autoinjector in CS7 occurred after the data cutoff for both the NDA and 90-day safety update and therefore no information is available.
L2714: The amount of exposure to the autoinjector is small. Refer to the Human Factors and Center for Devices and Radiological Health reviews for acceptability of this device.

L2723: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L2724: Two clinical site inspections and a Contract Research Organization (CRO) inspection were completed by the Office of Scientific Investigations. Based on the results of these inspections, Trials CS3 and CS8 appear to have been conducted adequately, and the clinical data generated by these sites appear to be reliable in support of this NDA.
L2725: The clinical sites were chosen using a risk-based approach primarily based on numbers of enrolled subjects, treatment effect, protocol deviations, and prior inspection history. The two sites included Seth Baum, M.D. (Site # 2138) and Alejandro De La Cruz, M.D. (Site # 3195).

L2753: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2754: The following postmarketing requirement is recommended:
L2755: Complete the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials (ISIS 678354-CS5 [CORE] and ISIS 678354-CS6 [CORE 2]) and the two-year open-label extension (OLE) trial (ISIS 678354-CS15) to evaluate the potential for drug induced liver injury with Tryngolza.
L2756: Interim Report Submission (ISIS 678354-CS5, ISIS 678354-CS6 trial data; OLE 678354-CS15 trial): June 2026
L2757: Interim Report Submission (OLE 678354-CS15 trial): June 2027
L2758: Interim Report Submission (OLE 678354-CS15 trial): June 2028
L2759: Trial Completion: December 2028
L2760: Final Report Submission: June 2029
L2761: All three trials should be completed by the trial completion milestone and the final report submission should include data from all three trials.
