L2855: Figure 1: A Schematic of ApoC-III Inhibition of Lipoprotein Transport. The diagram illustrates the role of ApoC-III in lipid metabolism and how its inhibition by olezarsen affects lipoprotein transport. Dietary fat is absorbed by the Intestine, leading to the formation of Chylomicrons (CM). CMs are transported to the Liver. ApoC-III is shown inhibiting the action of Lipoprotein Lipase (LPL) on CMs, leading to CM Remnants (CMR). CMRs are transported to the Liver. ApoC-III also inhibits the conversion of VLDL to IDL and IDL to LDL, and the uptake of LDL by the Liver via LDL receptors. The inhibition of ApoC-III (indicated by red arrows) leads to increased LPL activity, increased clearance of TG-rich lipoproteins, and increased hepatic uptake of TG-rich lipoproteins.
L2856: Figure 2 displays three plots showing predicted concentration-time profiles for 50 mg and 80 mg olezarsen administered subcutaneously (SC) every 4 weeks (Q4W). The left plot shows Olezarsen concentration (ng/mL) over time (Weeks), with the 80 mg dose achieving a higher steady-state concentration. The middle plot shows the percentage change from baseline in ApoC-III concentrations, and the right plot shows the percentage change from baseline in Triglycerides concentrations. Both ApoC-III and TG profiles show greater reduction for the 80 mg dose compared to the 50 mg dose.
L2857: Figure 3 displays two plots showing the simulated percent change from baseline in concentrations over time (Weeks 48 to 52) for two dosing regimens: 80 mg Q4W (every four weeks, red line) and 80 mg Q1M (every 30 days, blue line). The shaded areas represent the 95% Confidence Interval (CI) of the simulated median observations.
The left plot shows the % change from baseline in ApoC-III concentrations. Both regimens show a decrease in ApoC-III concentrations, with the Q1M regimen achieving a greater reduction (median around -75%) compared to Q4W (median around -70%) by Week 52.
The right plot shows the % change from baseline in Triglycerides concentrations. Both regimens show a significant decrease in Triglycerides concentrations, with the Q1M regimen achieving a greater reduction (median around -75%) compared to Q4W (median around -70%) by Week 52.
L2858: Figure 4 displays simulated pharmacokinetic (PK) metrics at steady-state across four doses (30 mg, 50 mg, 80 mg, 120 mg) and two dosing regimens (Q1M: every 30 days, Q4W: every four weeks). The figure consists of three box plots (A, B, C) comparing Q1M (red) and Q4W (teal) regimens.
A: C_{max,ss} (ng/mL): Maximum plasma concentration at steady state. C_{max,ss} increases significantly with dose for both regimens. At 120 mg, Q1M shows a median C_{max,ss} around 7000 ng/mL, while Q4W shows a median around 4000 ng/mL.
B: C_{trough,ss} (ng/mL): Trough plasma concentration at steady state. C_{trough,ss} increases with dose. At 120 mg, Q1M shows a median C_{trough,ss} around 15 ng/mL, while Q4W shows a median around 3 ng/mL.
C: AUC_{0-\infty,ss} (ng\cdoth/mL): Area under the concentration-time curve from time zero to infinity at steady state. AUC_{0-\infty,ss} increases substantially with dose. At 120 mg, Q1M shows a median AUC_{0-\infty,ss} around 10000 ng\cdoth/mL, while Q4W shows a median around 5000 ng\cdoth/mL.
Legend: Regimen Q1M (red) and Q4W (teal).
L2859: Figure 5 displays the percentage change from baseline of ApoC-III and Triglycerides (TG) at C_{trough,ss} across four doses (30 mg, 50 mg, 80 mg, 120 mg) and two dosing regimens (Q1M: every 30 days, Q4W: every four weeks). Both ApoC-III and TG show significant reductions (negative percentage change) with increasing dose and Q1M regimen.
ApoC-III Percentage Change from Baseline at C_{trough,ss} (%): The percentage change decreases (becomes more negative) as the dose increases. At 120 mg, Q1M shows a median percentage change around -75%, while Q4W shows a median around -50%.
TG Percentage Change from Baseline at C_{trough,ss} (%): The percentage change decreases (becomes more negative) as the dose increases. At 120 mg, Q1M shows a median percentage change around -75%, while Q4W shows a median around -50%.
Legend: Regimen Q1M (red) and Q4W (teal).

L2882: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis. The plot is divided into four quadrants by dashed lines at X=3 and Y=2.
Quadrant 1 (Top Left): Cholestasis (Bilirubin \ge 2 \times \text{ULN} and ALT/AST < 3 \times \text{ULN}).
Quadrant 2 (Top Right): Potential Hy's Law (Bilirubin \ge 2 \times \text{ULN} and ALT/AST \ge 3 \times \text{ULN}).
Quadrant 3 (Bottom Left): Temple's Corollary (Bilirubin < 2 \times \text{ULN} and ALT/AST \ge 3 \times \text{ULN}).
Quadrant 4 (Bottom Right): No liver injury screening criteria met.
Legend: Olezarsen 50mg (blue diamonds), Olezarsen 80mg (red squares), Placebo (white circles). Points circled in red indicate subjects meeting the 3X ULN criteria for ALT or AST within 30 days of the 2X ULN bilirubin criteria.
L2883: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis versus Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis. The plot is divided into four quadrants: Cholestasis (left upper), Potential Hy's Law (right upper), Temple's Corollary (right lower), and the remaining area (left lower). Data points are categorized by treatment group: Olezarsen 50mg (blue dots), Olezarsen 80mg (red squares), and Placebo (open circles). Vertical and horizontal dashed lines mark 3xULN and 2xULN thresholds, respectively. Points meeting both criteria are circled in red.

L2892: Figure 32 displays three scatter plots (A, B, and C) showing post hoc pharmacokinetic metrics at steady state versus Renal Function Category (Normal, Mild renal impairment, Moderate renal impairment). The sample sizes for each category are n=221, n=48, and n=3, respectively.
A: C_{\text{max,ss}} (ng/mL)
Y-axis: C_{\text{max,ss}} (ng/mL), ranging from 0 to 5000. The data points show a wide distribution, with the highest values observed in the Normal renal function group.
B: C_{\text{trough,ss}} (ng/mL)
Y-axis: C_{\text{trough,ss}} (ng/mL), ranging from 0 to 40. The data points are tightly clustered around 0 ng/mL across all renal function categories.

L2900: Figure 40 consists of two graphs, A and B, illustrating the concentration-dependent reduction of human apoC-III mRNA in primary transgenic mouse hepatocytes treated with ISIS 678354 (Olezarsen). The reduction increases significantly with increasing concentration, reaching approximately 100% reduction at 0.0005 µM and 100% reduction at 10 µM. Asterisks indicate statistical significance compared to UTC. Graph B is a dose response curve showing the mean percentage difference from UTC plus or minus SD versus olezarsen concentration on a logarithmic scale (0.0001 to 100 µM). The curve shows a dose-dependent reduction, with the percentage difference decreasing as the concentration increases.
L2901: Bar chart showing Liver ApoC-III Expression (% saline & SD) in Cynomolgus Monkeys at Week 13 (D93), Week 39 (D275), and Week 65 (D455) for saline and olezarsen doses (2, 6, 12, and 30 mg/kg/wk). At Week 65, expression recovers significantly in the 30 mg/kg/wk group.
L2902: Figure 42 displays two graphs showing the exposure profile curve of ISIS 678354-equivalent concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) versus Time Point (h) in mice, comparing doses of 2 mg/kg (black squares) and 10 mg/kg (blue circles). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.0001 to 100.
Day 1: Both doses show rapid absorption, peaking around 4 hours. The 10 mg/kg dose achieves a higher peak concentration (approx. 8 \mu\text{g/mL}) compared to the 2 mg/kg dose (approx. 1 \mu\text{g/mL}). Both doses exhibit a prolonged clearance phase, with concentrations stabilizing around 0.01 \mu\text{g/mL} by 48 hours.
Day 42: The profiles are similar to Day 1, indicating steady-state conditions. The 10 mg/kg dose maintains a higher steady-state concentration (approx. 0.05 \mu\text{g/mL}) compared to the 2 mg/kg dose (approx. 0.01 \mu\text{g/mL}).
L2903: Figure 43 displays six graphs showing the Mean ISIS 678354 equivalent concentration (eq conc.) in Plasma (\mu\text{g/mL}) versus Time Point (h) for six different groups of monkeys, illustrating the pharmacokinetic profile of ISIS 678354.
The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.001 to 1000 \mu\text{g/mL}. The X-axis represents Time Point (h) from 0 to 48.
The graphs are arranged in three rows and two columns:
Group 2, 2 mg/kg: Compares Day 1 (solid line), Day 91 (dashed line), and Day 273 (dotted line). Day 1 shows the highest peak concentration (around 10 \mu\text{g/mL}) which rapidly declines. Day 91 and Day 273 show significantly lower and sustained concentrations (around 0.1 \mu\text{g/mL}).
Group 3, 6 mg/kg: Compares Day 1, Day 91, and Day 273. Day 1 shows the highest peak concentration (around 10 \mu\text{g/mL}). Day 91 and Day 273 show lower and sustained concentrations (around 0.1 \mu\text{g/mL}).
Group 4, 12 mg/kg: Compares Day 1, Day 91, and Day 273. Day 1 shows the highest peak concentration (around 10 \mu\text{g/mL}). Day 91 and Day 273 show lower and sustained concentrations (around 0.1 \mu\text{g/mL}).
Group 5, 30 mg/kg: Compares Day 1 and Day 91. Day 1 shows the highest peak concentration (around 10 \mu\text{g/mL}). Day 91 shows a lower and sustained concentration (around 0.1 \mu\text{g/mL}).
Group 6, 2 mg/kg: Compares Day 1 and Day 7. Day 1 shows the highest peak concentration (around 10 \mu\text{g/mL}). Day 7 shows a lower and sustained concentration (around 0.1 \mu\text{g/mL}).
In all groups, the concentration peaks rapidly after administration (around 4-8 hours) and then declines slowly, indicating a long half-life. Higher doses generally result in higher peak concentrations.

L2915: Figure 53 displays two scatter plots showing the percent change from baseline concentration over time (days) for Olezarsen 50 mg Q4W, stratified by ADA status (ADA Negative and ADA Positive).
The X-axis represents Time (day), ranging from 0 to 364. The Y-axis represents the Percent Change From Baseline Concentration (%).
The top panel shows the %\Delta ApoC-III Concentration (%). The bottom panel shows the %\Delta Triglyceride Concentration (%).
The legend indicates:
ADA Status: ADA Negative (Red line and dots) and ADA Positive (Blue line and dots).
In the Top Panel (%\Delta ApoC-III Concentration), both groups show a decrease in ApoC-III concentration over time, with the ADA Negative group generally showing a greater reduction (more negative percentage change) than the ADA Positive group.
In the Bottom Panel (%\Delta Triglyceride Concentration), both groups show a decrease in Triglyceride concentration over time, with the ADA Negative group generally showing a greater reduction (more negative percentage change) than the ADA Positive group.
L2916: Figure 54 displays two scatter plots showing the Percent Change From Baseline (PCFB) of ApoC-III (Top Panel) and Triglyceride (TG) (Bottom Panel) concentration over time (day) for Olezarsen 80 mg Q4W, Trial CS3, stratified by ADA Status (ADA Negative vs. ADA Positive).
The X-axis represents Time (day) from 0 to 364. The Y-axis represents %\Delta Concentration (%), ranging from -100% to 100%.
The legend indicates: ADA Status (ADA Negative: Red line, ADA Positive: Blue line).
Top Panel: %\Delta ApoC-III Concentration (%)
ADA Negative (Red line): PCFB generally ranges between -50% and 0%. It shows a slight decrease from approximately -40% at day 28 to around -50% at day 112, and then stabilizes around -50% to -60%.
ADA Positive (Blue line): PCFB is highly variable, ranging from approximately -100% to 0%. It shows significant fluctuations, often reaching -100% around day 28 and day 308.
Bottom Panel: %\Delta Triglyceride Concentration (%)
ADA Negative (Red line): PCFB generally ranges between -50% and 0%. It shows a slight decrease from approximately -40% at day 28 to around -50% at day 112, and then stabilizes around -50% to -60%.
ADA Positive (Blue line): PCFB is highly variable, ranging from approximately -100% to 0%. It shows significant fluctuations, often reaching -100% around day 28 and day 308.
L2917: Figure 55 displays four plots assessing the goodness of fit of the final PK model for olezarsen sodium (Tryngolza).
The top row shows Observed concentrations (ng/mL) versus Population predictions (ng/mL) and Individual Predictions (ng/mL). Both plots use a log10-log10 scale for the axes. The data points are clustered around the line of unity (black line, y=x), and a red line represents a loess smooth curve, indicating a good fit.
The bottom row shows Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) versus Population predictions (ng/mL) and Time after first dose (hours). The CWRES plots show residuals centered around zero (red line, y=0), with a loess smooth curve (red line) indicating no systematic bias. The residuals are generally within the range of -4 to 4.
L2918: Figure 56A displays the Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Check (VPC) for Plasma Olezarsen Concentration (ng/mL) versus Time Since Last Dose (h) for subjects with ADA negative status. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.1 to 10000 ng/mL. The X-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.3 to 3000 h. Observed individual concentrations are shown as open circles. The model predicted median concentration is shown as a solid line. The 5th and 95th percentiles of observed data are shown as dashed lines. The 95% confidence interval around the model predicted 5th, 50th, and 95th percentiles is shown as shaded areas (blue and red/orange). The plot shows that the model predictions generally align well with the observed data across the entire time range, indicating good predictive performance for ADA negative subjects.

L2925: SC Dose: 50 mg Q4W 80 mg Q4W
Figure 62 displays two panels showing Olezarsen concentration (ng/mL) versus Time (Weeks) for 50 mg Q4W (red/orange) and 80 mg Q4W (blue/cyan) doses. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.5 to 800.0 ng/mL. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 52 weeks.
The left panel, labeled "First dose," shows the concentration profile immediately following the initial injection (Time 0). Both doses exhibit a rapid peak concentration (around 700-800 ng/mL) followed by a quick decline. The 80 mg Q4W dose maintains a higher steady-state concentration than the 50 mg Q4W dose.
The right panel, labeled "Steady-state," shows the concentration profile after repeated dosing. Both doses show overlapping concentration profiles, indicating that the 80 mg Q4W dose achieves a marginally higher steady-state concentration compared to the 50 mg Q4W dose.
L2926: Figure 62 continued displays four plots showing the percentage change from baseline in concentrations over time (Weeks) for two doses (50 mg Q4W and 80 mg Q4W). The shaded areas represent the 95% CI of the simulated median observations obtained from Monte-Carlo simulations.
The top row shows results for ApoC-III concentrations, and the bottom row shows results for Triglycerides concentrations.
Top Row: ApoC-III concentrations
First dose (Weeks 0 to 4): Both doses show a rapid decrease in ApoC-III concentrations, reaching approximately -50% to -75% change from baseline by Week 4. The 80 mg Q4W dose generally shows a greater reduction.
Steady-state (Weeks 48 to 52): Both doses show a slight increase in ApoC-III concentrations compared to the first dose period, stabilizing around -50% to -75% change from baseline.
Bottom Row: Triglycerides concentrations
First dose (Weeks 0 to 4): Both doses show a rapid decrease in Triglycerides concentrations, reaching approximately -50% to -75% change from baseline by Week 4. The 80 mg Q4W dose generally shows a greater reduction.
Steady-state (Weeks 48 to 52): Both doses show a slight increase in Triglycerides concentrations compared to the first dose period, stabilizing around -50% to -75% change from baseline.
Legend: SC Dose: 50 mg Q4W (Red/Pink), 80 mg Q4W (Blue/Cyan).

L2928: Figure 64A displays two scatter plots showing the Change from Baseline in Platelet Count (\times 10^9/L) versus Cumulative AUC (ng.h/mL), stratified by study (ISIS 678354-CS3 and ISIS 678354-CS8). L2939: Figure 71 is a waterfall plot showing the Mean (and 95% CI) Change From Baseline Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL) over time for three treatment arms in Trial CS3. The X-axis represents time points from Week 1 to Week 53, including follow-up periods. The Y-axis represents the Mean Change from Baseline Fasting Triglycerides (mg/dL), ranging from -2000 to 1000.
The legend indicates the treatment arms:
ISIS 678354 80 mg (Blue line)
ISIS 678354 50 mg (Orange line)
Pooled Placebo (Black line)
The plot shows that the Pooled Placebo group generally maintained a mean change near zero. The ISIS 678354 80 mg group showed a significant decrease in fasting triglycerides, reaching approximately -1500 mg/dL by Week 27. The ISIS 678354 50 mg group also showed a decrease, reaching approximately -1000 mg/dL by Week 27. Both active treatment groups maintained lower mean triglyceride levels compared to the placebo group for the remainder of the study period.
A secondary bar chart below the main plot shows the Number of Records (N) for each analysis visit, ranging from 0 to 25. The number of records is generally high (around 20-25) during the treatment period (Weeks 1-53) and drops significantly during the follow-up period (Weeks 54-57).
